It's important to coldly examine your assumptions and interpretations when looking at data because your intuition can often be wrong.  Also, Math is Fun.  Counterintuitive math, facts. Quote education has made all the difference for me. It builds software for your brain,  Mathematics taught me to reason logically and understand numbers tables charts and calculations even more. Valuable I learned at an early age to teach myself and quote  Edward Thorp a man for all markets.  1 of the most fun parts of math is that even though it's 100% objective and rational, it can still surprise us and it can still sometimes feel a little bit like magic.  Part of acquiring a higher level in rational understanding of math and probability is to Simply understand that math. Statistics and probability are not always 100% intuitive.  Many empirical and statistical outcomes can be surprising.  In this section, I'll take a look at a few instances, not so much because these specific examples are important for trading.  But more. So you can see that it's worth challenging your system 1 assumptions. If you want to get to the right, mathematical answers Alpha Traders, have a strong basic understanding of numbers and statistics.  You should constantly be working to improve your numeracy.  The counterintuitive, math, facts that follow highlight, just how wrong your gut can be when it jumps to conclusions and should reinforce that your slow. Rational mind is where it's at.  Example, 1 a and 1 B, the bat and ball problem.  The classic counterintuitive, math problem is the bat and ball problem.  If you've not heard this before, take a quick second to answer, the question is a baseball bat and a ball cost A110 total. The bat costs a dollar more than the ball. How much does the ball cost?  Did you answer 10 cents?  that's the standard system 1 answer, but if you stop to think for just a moment, To see the answer is 5 cents.  This is question 1 on the cognitive reflection test discussed earlier. It shows how if we listen to our gut, we get the wrong answer, our brains can take data and jump to seemingly logical but completely wrong conclusions. Slow down and use system 2 for mathematical analysis. Evaluate alternative hypothesis. Even when the answer seems obvious,  When it comes to trading, the more obvious, the conclusion, the more likely it is already priced into the market and thus, the more likely the trade you enter based on the conclusion will lose money.  Another quick 1, you have thousand dollars. Split evenly between 10, stocks. They all drop 1%. What? Percentage of your portfolio. Did you just lose in total? Most people get that 1 write. The answer is 1%, but they hear the wrong answer in their head first. 10%,  again, another example of 2 systems, operating at the same time, inside your head  Example, 2 up. Down up down, is actually down, $100 stock goes down. 10%, 1 day. Then up. 10% the next. What's the stock worth now?  If you answered 100 dollars, you use system 1 and you are wrong.  If you answered 99 you use system 2 and you're right.  This is another simple example of a mathematical result. That is not always obvious on first inspection. If you ask someone, what happens to a stock that goes down, 20% 1 day, then up, 20% the next their knee-jerk or system. 1 response would be that the stock is unchanged.  But anyone that worked at Lehman Brothers in 2008 knows that if something goes down 20% Then up 20% over and over. It will eventually end up much much lower and there's a Nifty chart in the PDF  This truth can be painful in trading, if you lose 10% of your Capital, you need to make 11.1% to get back to Flat. If you drop 50% of your Capital, you need to double to Recover, if you lose your mind and drop 90% of your Capital, you'll need to make 1,000% just to get back to Flat the further you fall from your starting Capital, the harder it will be to ever come back.  Example, 3, the birthday paradox.  Here's a simple 1. There are 70 people in a room. What are the odds that 2 of them will have the same birthday?  If you don't know this problem, you might start with 70 divided by 365 and realize it's much higher than that. So maybe guess like 50% 60%.  How about 99.9%?  Most people's intuition is to start with 1 birthday and think through the odds of someone in the room having that birthday. But really all you need is 1 of any of 70 birthdays to match any other birthday in the room.  And in the PDF, there's a chart and it shows that basically once you get past 23 people, you already add a 50/50 shot of 2 matching birthdays.  I learned this. How many years ago and it still gives me a mild case of vertigo when I think about it. It's weirdly counterintuitive.  When you analyze Market data and come to conclusions, make sure you're confident that your conclusions are logical. Not just intuitive, logical, even if something seems obvious check it think about it.  The next example is nonlinear craziness, when you go to a casino, you know, you have a good chance of losing. But do you truly appreciate how your Equity evolves over time in an environment with negative leakage? Think about this, if you start the night with a hundred dollars and play a fair game, 50% chance of winning at $1 to win. $1, what are the odds you'll hit plus 100 before you lose your 100?  Uh, trick question because it's not a trick 50%.  Okay. Now, what are the odds you get to 100 before losing 100 in the same 1 dollar game, but now the odds of winning are 49.5%  The answer is only 11. .5% of the time you'll get to plus 100 before minus 100.  What about if the odds in the game are 49%, then you only win a hundred dollars 1.8% of the time.  As you repeat a game with Negative Edge, the probability of loss is nonlinear, this is why the only way to make money at a casino is with highly variable or lumpy bets. If you want maximum odds to win in Vegas, go to the roulette table when you arrive put your entire stake on black or red,  Another strategy is to place. Lumpy Bets with your 100 dollars. Bet $50 then $2. $60 and $1 at least this way you have a chance. Any methodical application of a strategy that has a sub 50% win rate will lead to almost guaranteed loss.  When I'm trading badly, my p&l kind of looks like the $1 bet with the 49% odds.  Note that this is just an example of how math can be counterintuitive. Takeaway should not be that you want a randomly, make lumpy bet. Concentrating in trading your bet. Sizing, should eliminate risk of Ruin, be proportionate to conviction, level be large enough. Even if it's a low conviction, trade to move the needle towards your goal.  Finally note that if your capital is slowly bleeding lower over an extended period, it is a sign that you have Negative Edge.  In the PDF. There's a chart of 10 simulations of a 4852 game where you risk 1 to make 1 dollar. If your p&l chart has that steady top left to bottom, right? Skew, like the simulation. It's time to think about your Edge as it disappeared have, markets changed and you did not adapt. Are you paying too much in transaction costs?  The next example is survivors and incomplete samples. People don't always think about whether their sample is complete or biased.  In World War II. The US Navy, wanted to reduce the number of its fighter planes, being shot down and wanted to add armor. At various points to reinforce the fighters, armor increases a plane's weight, and makes it less efficient. So, you need to be selective about where to put armor. The statisticians. In the wartime, statistical research group received a diagram showing data, with a whole bunch of bullet holes, all over the place on the wings, the center of the fuselage and the tail.  The diagram is in the PDF. So the intuitive or obvious answer was to reinforce the areas that are getting hit by enemy fire, the wings fuselage and tail. Look like natural places to add armor since they get shot up the most  but legendary mathematician, Abraham Wald, ignored the intuitive answer and thought more deeply. He thought to ask, why are there so many holes in the fuselage and wings but no bullet holes in the engines.  This is like asking why are there so many, ER, patients with gunshot wounds to the arm. But so few with gunshot wounds to the heart even though both sections of the human, Body are in a similar area.  The answer of course, is that the planes that got shot in, the engine went down and were destroyed, so they couldn't be examined.  There are no bullet holes in the engines of the planes that return to base because the planes that are shot in the engine, don't make it back to base victims, shot in the heart, don't make it to the ER,  Survivorship bias is the phenomenon where we evaluate a sample say planes returning from Battle or mutual funds.  And then draw conclusions based on that sample with without considering all the parts of the sample that did not survive, we look at planes that return to base without considering the ones that got shot down.  We look at performance of existing mutual funds without considering all the ones that went out of business, this is another way we misinterpret data.  Here's 1 way this applies to trading a friend of yours shows. You a specific strategy that earned 1,155 returns last month. Your first reaction might be. Wow, tell me more. When really you're First reaction should be, what other strategies did you test before coming up with this 1? When analyzing data look for missing evidence, this is a common trick in the sports. Gambling industry, say the tigers are playing the Bears tonight. You send 10,000 emails at to new prospects. 5,000 of the emails, read attention, subscribers, gold, star guaranteed pick. Tonight is Tigers to win and 5,000 say, attention, subscribers are gold, star are guaranteed pick. Tonight, is Bears to win.  Then they wait to see who wins. Let's say it's the Tigers.  The next game is Lions versus Panthers.  The marketer now takes the list of 5,000 emails that received the correct. Tigers pick email and splits those into 2 groups of 2500, half the emails, predict the Lions and have predict the Panthers.  They do this twice more. Now, they have a list of 625 people that just received 4 straight, gold star, pick winners, then they call up those 600. 25 prospects, many of whom are suitably impressed. Nobody else had the Panthers to beat the lions in that game.  And try to close the deal on a 1200, a year subscription to the gold star pick sheet.  Those betters that got the 4 Acura emails in a row. Now have to be pretty smart to think of the Hidden evidence. They might be missing more likely their emotional desire to win. Overrides their logical or rational mind and they hand their credit card. Number over to the waiting operator  Numbers on their own. Do not always tell the full story. You need to think more deeply about conclusions and treat. Even the most obvious answers with rational skepticism remember. Skepticism is not cynicism, it doesn't mean you assume everything is wrong and everyone's lying and nothing is true.  Alpha Traders filter information with slow system 2 Thinking. She listens to experts but does not take their claims or forecasts at face value. She uses research and logic to see through false claims and avoid Hasty incorrect conclusions. Understand liquidity, some products like FX are extremely liquid, and trade continuously all day and all week, some markets, like Lumber and wheat are much less liquid. While some futures Contracts can hit limit up or limit down for multiple days in a row leaving specs trapped on the wrong side.  How does your product trade is there? Plenty of liquidity all the time or do you need to consider liquidity when you execute?  Let's drill into liquidity, a bit. So you can think about the specific features of your Market  Transaction costs and bid ask spread.  The difference between the bid and the ask in a market is called the spread.  Transaction costs are the amount of spread you pay when you trade.  Spread time number of units. Equals transaction cost.  If the bid and apple is 350 and the ask is $150.88, the spread is 8 cents. This is often expressed either as a percentage of the mid price or in basis points.  So 8 cents divided by 3504.  Is 0.029, which is 2.9 basis points.  A basis point is 1/100. Of 1%. Therefore for example 1% is 100 basis points.  2.9 basis points is 1 of the tightest bid offer spreads, you will ever see.  Spreads very dramatically by asset class. And by product within an asset class in FX spreads in euro dollar, the most liquid pair are often less than 1/10 of spreads in dollar turkey, the Turkish Lera.  The bid ask in a small cap or micro cap, stock could be 50 times. The Apple bid ask gold trades way, tighter than Palladium, spreads in credit, are generally wider than spreads, in fixed income, spreads in agricultural. Commodities are generally wider than spreads in short-term interest rate, Futures and so on.  Every Market has a different texture and part of this. Texture comes from the bid offer spread trading activity. On each side of the spread is called, bid, ask bounce.  Think about your average trade size and call that 1 unit. How much does it cost you to trade 1 unit in normal markets? for example, if you're a normal trade size is 10 million euro dollar and Euro dollar trades, 2 Pips wide, and 10 million, so 1 pip equals 0.001,  Each round-trip buy and sell cost and dollars.  That is to say if the market in euro dollar is bid at 1:10, 000 and offered at 1:10 02 and you buy and sell without the market moving you paid 0.002.  In transaction costs, which is $0 per 10 million transaction.  Don't worry too much about the math here. These are just examples.  When you buy and sell stocks at zero commission, you're still paying the toll of somewhere between 0.02% and 0.5% that is 2 to 50 basis points.  Due to the bid ask spread.  Some stocks are much wider than 50 basis points, but you probably should not be trading those.  Spreads and commissions are the main reason trading is a negative sum game. Not a zero sum Spread is the invisible enemy like a slow leak in a tire. It costs you a ton of p&l but it can be very hard to observe or measure correctly.  Tiffany is an active day trader. She executes 15 round trips per day, 15 buy and 15 sells and the stock she specializes in Take 2, interactive has an average bid ask of 38 basis points.  That means if the Stock's trading at 173, the average bid ask is about 66 cents.  Her account size is 100,000 and her average trade size is 150 shares.  So she's leaking 150 shares times 15 round trips times 66 cents per round trip which is 1,485 per day times 5 days, that's 7,425 per week times, 48 weeks, that's 356,400 per year, insane, right? These are realistic assumptions and give you a good sense. Just how expensive it can be to cross big spreads.  She can reduce her transaction costs by using other execution strategies like, limit orders, but we don't know if this will hurt her performance. Obviously, she can't keep doing this for very long unless her strategy has significant Edge. If she use the same strategy, but switched over to Apple and we assumed 3 basis points bid asking Apple, her transaction costs dropped to 117 per day 585 per week or 28,000 per year. Now, I'm not saying, you should only trade Apple what I'm saying is that you should have a clear and thorough understanding of how much it costs you to transact. When you fully grow, how expensive it is to transact, you will probably trade less as we have discussed transacting, less is almost always a good thing.  Accurate data on bid offer, spreads can be hard to come by.  In FX, the bank's collect this data and some are willing to share it with clients While, others view it, as proprietary. And Stock market, the easiest thing to do is watch the stock you trade and take occasional snapshots of the spread.  Bid offer spreads in an individual stock will vary substantially by time of day and around events in times of stress. It offer, spreads can widen dramatically, but in normal markets, they're somewhat consistent. In other words, if Apple trades, 3 basis points, wide today at 10, a.m. it will probably trade around 3 basis points wide tomorrow. At 10 a.m. unless there's news or a profound change in the markets,  In contrast at the open 9:30 a.m. it could be tighter or wider. And at times of higher volatility, it will almost certainly trade wider your estimate of bid offer. Spreads does not have to be perfect, just eyeball it and multiply by your estimated number of Trades per day to get a rough sense of your transaction costs or TC.  TC is a slow and steady League that adds up substantially.  you need a significant Edge to make money in any Market because you don't just need to generate Alpha, you need to generate Alpha net of transaction costs.  In the PDF I have 4 charts that show. The theoretical, p&l for 2 different Traders, the thin Grey Line shows the p&l before transaction costs and the black line shows p&l after TC or net.  I used realistic bid offer spreads to show the impact of trading costs on 2 real estate p&l streams so you can get a sense of the enormous strain TC exerts on performance.  You should not obsess over TC, but you need to be smart about it.  Most of all you need to quantify and monitor it. TC is a serious and important League that you need to reduce as much as possible.  At the same time TC is a cost of doing business and trading and you don't want an unhealthy approach to reducing TC that could jeopardize your reputation or your future liquidity be smart, but not obsessive about reducing TC. We'll discuss some ways to do this in the section on execution, Chapter 12.  Gap risk.  Another element of liquidity is Gap risk. There are 3 types of Gap risk. The first is when markets closed and then reopen, a stock closes at $50 on Friday. And then over the weekend, their CFO quits, and the company announces. They fired, their auditor that doesn't sound good. The stock opens at twenty 5 dollars on Monday morning. Yikes, if you have a 50,000 account and you went home long at 2x margin, you're done ruined, that's gap risk. Number 1,  Usually, the timing of closed to open Gap risk. Like, that can be predicted, in advance, since we know the trading hours of whatever security we trade, but this is not 100% the case many features have trading limits. If they move a certain amount trading is halted,  individual stocks are often halted for pending news or due to extreme volatility. You must have a complete understanding of how your Market functions and avoid. Risk of Ruin before everything else.  The second type of Gap risk relates to events when the US jobs report comes out the first Friday of each month at 8:30, a.m. you'll often see S&P futures and Len and other markets, jump to a new level without trading continuously from A to B.  For example, Delian might be trading at 112 at 82929.  And then the jobs number comes in strong and the first trade after 8:30 a.m. could be 1 12608. If you go into the number short and don't have a good understanding of the Gap risk, you might get wrecked.  After economic releases and events, there's usually a fast market for 5 or ten minutes as well. So not only do you need to worry about Gap risk but also liquidity risk, even if your trading small size, it can be difficult to get in and out of fast markets. You might deal on wrong prices or get error messages or if you trade bigger size simply find you cannot transact your full amount.  Like I said earlier, unless you think you have a big Edge on the number or the event, go in flat and react.  The third type of Gap risk comes from markets that are pegged capped, or floored by central banks, or other entities. When famous example of this is the Euro Swiss floor at 120, the Swiss National Bank tried to enforce an exchange rate. And eventually when they gave up the largest 1-day move in the history of Foreign Exchange happen in about 2 hours Euro, Swiss traded from 1.2 to 0.85 in a matter of minutes that is absolutely insane.  Pegs and caps and floors are most common in FX markets, but also show up in equity markets in the days, after an IPO.  Market makers, representing the investment banks that underwrote the IPO are permitted to intervene in the market, to stabilize the price of the stock if it starts to fall below the IPO price.  This stabilization bit is not infinite. At some point, the underwriter will say Enough is enough and pull away and the stock will usually Gap aggressively lower.  There's a fantastic piece about the Facebook IPO stabilization effort on the Liberty Street, economics blog.  In the PDF, there's a chart that shows the number of shares on the bid, which are the vertical bars and may increase as Facebook shares price reached $38, which was the IPO price on its first day of trading.  Huge bids came in from the underwriter of the Facebook IPO.  The chart shows that the underwriter made a concerted effort to buy nearly infinite stock at 38 Traders, might see this huge intervention and think it's smart to go long with the stop below 38. Generally, that is not the right way to look at situations like this.  Instead, you should realize that the price is badly out of equilibrium.  With 1 buy and Myriad Cellars. The stock is out of line. And the second, the buyer disappears, it's going to be chaos as the price will Gap lower in search of equilibrium.  The second chart on the same page of the p. Shows the first day of trading. Plus the next 5 days, when the stock opened the day, after the IPO, the stabilization bid wasn't their Facebook got lower? Lungs were lucky. If they got out at 34, the stock did not trade back above 38 again for more, than a year.  Well, it's tempting to go with the market Distortion, Like the Facebook stabilization bid or the SMB bid neuroses at 120.  Counterintuitively, the best move is often to go against it.  When you follow an intervention bid, you're dead. If that bid disappears, if you go short against it and the price rallies, there will probably be a reasonable price where you can get out.  When an entity is supporting the price of a security or currency pair, it is usually reasonable to assume that that price is out of equilibrium and the entity supporting it is the only source of demand unless you have an edge in predicting. What that entity is going to do. You should stay away these setups present, huge gap risk and can lead to risk of ruin. Many retail investors and some large well-known hedge funds were ruined by the SNB fail at 120.  Is it worth betting, your job, your client's capital, or your entire fund on a single trade? No, never.  When Gap, brisk is Extreme or hard to estimate avoid it, the risk is never worth the reward.  Be thoughtful about major Gap risks in your product and avoid them.  This does not mean you can never carry a position through economic data, gaps around, economic data can be reasonably estimated. What it does mean is you should not go short, a tech bubble stock in earnings. Market professionals. Often call, volatility v v is a critical part of a Market's micro structure. It's the dispersion of returns over time. Usually measured as the standard deviation of logarithmic returns in simple English. How much does the thing? Move volatility is not constant or static a currency pair, like dollar. Yen might realize 6% volatility 1 year and 18%. The next Vol is a key concept to understand and you should always know your products. Vol profile. How that is evolved over time and how that might change in the future. A few things you need to know about vol. 1 markets tend to be more volatile around events because equilibrium is a function of all known information, and events. Introduce new information and upset. The equilibrium. For example, overnight V for Aussie dollar, might be 8% on a Normal day and 20% on The Day, The Reserve Bank of Australia meets Facebook trades, with higher, V on earnings day, relative to other days.  2. Volatile, if Equity volatility is exploding higher, it's likely that FX bond market and commodities Vault will go up too. There's often a single Factor driving volatility in capital markets. This can be economic uncertainty, geopolitical drama or something else. Volatility and uncertainty are usually associated.  And 3, it's hard to make money, trading low volume markets, most Traders, make more money when things are moving, 1 of the keys to success in trading is identifying the V regime and adjusting your trading accordingly. If you notice a meaningful drop in volatility in something, you trade, consider trading something else or monitor the situation closely and see if your p&l is drifting lower along with v.  To be 100% clear. When people talk about volatility, they usually mean historical volatility, but it's important to understand the 2 different types of vole  Historical volatility is the dispersion of returns in the past. You can measure it different ways, but unless you're an options Trader, I wouldn't worry about where the number comes from. Just know that when people say volatility, they're mostly referring to daily historical or realized volatility the amount. The security has moved around in the past based on daily data.  Implied volatility.  This is the market price for volatility, going forward. This measure is used in options pricing and it's a better proxy for future volatility than historical volatility. The distinction is not important here. Here and for the purposes of an exercise like V adjusted position sizing, it rarely matters which data you use. When it comes to the market. You trade, what is the average daily range for your product?  Daily, average ranges are useful, and it's also good to know. The average hourly range and how that range varies by time of day. When thinking about volatility, it's important to understand the current regime but you should also know how volatility has changed. Changed over time. If you're long on earnings day, you should modify your parameters relative to a normal day to account for the higher expected volatility.  most Traders would know that a stock is more volatile on earnings day, but when you crunch the data, you get a deeper level of understanding  If you catch a positive earnings move in apple, it makes sense to leave a few limit orders around yesterday's clothes plus 7 to 10%, because that's about as big a move as you can expect based on historical data.  As usual, this type of study gives you Clues, not answers. It filters out noise and lets you focus on the meaningful changes in price. There is no time element in a point and figure chart. It simply measures price changes of a certain size. This is called the price interval. The x-axis is blank.  In the PDF, I show a point and figure chart I made for Tesla on December 18th 2020. The day Tesla was added to the S&P 500. Step 2 towards becoming an expert in your Market.  This chapter will explain how to The study narrative and fundamentals and how to use this knowledge to better forecast short-term Market Direction.  Excellent Traders have an expert level knowledge of the primary narrative and competing sub narratives that drive their Market.  They understand the importance of the story, the market is telling and can see hear and feel when that story is changing, they know what is common knowledge and what is not.  quote, in my whole life, I've known no wise people who didn't read all the time, none zero,  I've gotten paid a lot over the years for reading through the newspapers end quote. Charlie Munger  Once you understand the micro structure of your market and the basics of who moves the market and how and when it moves you should work on your understanding of what moves it and why the what and the why of Market movements are never simple. And as you gain experience, you will realize more and more that many explanations in the financial press. Our way off base.  The Narrative is the story. The Market tells itself to explain movements in price,  like, any good story, there's usually the main plot and then a few subplots. The main Arc is the most important to understand, because markets tend to focus on 1 thing at a time.  But you also need to be ready for the next story as it elbows, today's narrative out of the way.  Here's how Bruce Conor explains it in Market Wizards quote.  1 of the jobs of a good Trader is to imagine alternative scenarios. I try to form many mental pictures of what the world should be like, and wait for 1 of them to be confirmed. You keep trying them on 1 at a time. Inevitably, most of these pictures will turn out to be wrong. That is only a few elements of the picture may prove, correct. But then all of a sudden you'll find that in 1 picture 9 out of 10 elements, click that scenario, then becomes your image of the world reality end quote.  Understanding Nar. Is what most people used to call fundamental analysis, but there's an important Nuance narrative. Analysis is about studying, not just what you think is the story. But more importantly, what story Market players are telling. And what story The Market itself is trying to tell  When it is done, well, narrative. Analysis is a coherent and fluid combination of everchanging, macroeconomic fundamentals, and behavioral factors.  It's often about nailing down the 1, most important story. The Market wants to trade.  For example, 2010 to 2012 was a remarkable period in FX markets. As the market watched, the near implosion of the Euro Zone at the same time as the US Federal Reserve enacted, a series of quantitative easing QE programs.  The Eurozone crisis was bearish Euro and US QE was bearish the dollar during this period euro dollar was known as 2 garbani. Ating that period though was that 1 story or the other clearly dominated most of the time.  The market was either freaking out about the Euro zone or going bananas over us QE.  But it was usually clear which theme was in the driver's seat. This is typical  It's very difficult for markets to hold 2 competing themes in its Collective hive mind at once. So the focus oscillates back and forth instead of going in 2 directions at the same time,  Understanding The Narrative Cycles important, because when you're good at it, it's a critical tool that helps you identify turning points and trends.  You see the psychology and storytelling at work in the market and you can predict where price is going to go.  Surprisingly, The Narrative often turns before price because price has momentum of its own and a propensity to overshoot.  Speculators invested in a trend have confirmation bias and will ignore the changing narrative until the turn in price forces them to pay attention.  Those turning points when the narrative is changed but price. Remains in the old Trend can be some of the most exciting and profitable moments in trading.  In the PDF, I show a stylized view of the typical narrative cycle.  The chart is similar to other cycle analysis, like the Panic Euphoria model or the famous sell sell, sell buy, buy, buy comic by Cal.  It also runs a fair bit with Elliot wave.  Understand the narrative cycle.  It's important to note, when listening to this, that the narrative cycle operates on many times fractals. There are the big macroeconomic narratives, but there are also sometimes much shorter narrative cycles, that can last as little as 24 hours.  Often news or economic data will appear to be important, but then run out of steam, very swiftly as the market pivots back to the main story.  Picture a web of interconnected narratives with 1 or 2 dominating and the rest, all swirling around and buying for attention. The goal is to Determine what stories are driving your market now and what stories will jockey for position and come out ahead in the future?  Traders with more experience, will recognize the stage of the narrative intuitively and will often have a strong sense of when a story is running out of gas as a signposts are often similar at various stages of the narrative cycle.  Ven hunt of Epsilon theory is 1 of the kings of understanding parsing, and forecasting The Narrative or Zeitgeist I strongly recommend you check out his material if you want to excel in narrative analysis.  each stage of every narrative cycle will look and feel familiar in many ways even when the stories themselves will be radically different each time, kind of like a Hollywood movie that follows a standard 3-act Arc but with different characters setting and plot  Let's go through the 7 stages of the narrative cycle as described in the chart in the PDF.  Stage 1 under the radar.  At this point. There's a new narrative forming but very few Traders, have heard about it, some smart independent thinkers, and research-oriented people are talking about it and a few contrarians are putting on the position.  There are usually a few pullbacks as initial Believers have low confidence Traders, testing the waters tend to get bored or jiggled out because the price is not moving their way or they just figure they're too early and don't want to waste time on a narrative. That's not valid.  The Holy Grail of trading of course is to identify below the radar stories that will eventually become important Market narratives. You want to be early to the good stories but if you're too early you need to make sure you don't get bored and give up before the market catches on to the theme.  Stage 2, momentum builds. At this point, the narrative goes a bit viral at least among professionals or experts and price jumps or Falls to reflect the new zeitgeist.  my narrative cycle chart shows a bullish story driving the price of an asset upward, but you can flip the whole thing for about news narrative as the price jumps often on a particular piece of news that suddenly clarifies what was previously still, a foggy narrative people take notice  remember that at every stage of the narrative cycle, there is a dynamic interplay between price and narrative. The story drives a price and the price drives the story and often it's hard to tell, which 1 is causing the movement. For example, say a stock has a good story but it's still in stage 1 of The Narrative cycle, nobody's paying attention.  A fun manager reads, some 3 week old research and decides he loves the story, he starts buying stock and liquidity is poor the price rallies 4%, some stale shorts, stub out and the fund manager keeps buying soon the stocks up 11% over the course of 2 days.  because the stock rallied it appears on a few Radars, many firms and individuals run scans for stocks with unusual volatility in this generates some new interest Traders and investors look it up and Some of them. Like what? They see great story that triggers more buying in this little description. The price is now, feeding the narrative, not the other way around. The narrative already existed 3 weeks ago.  As momentum builds and the stock hits, for example, a 60-day high Trend followers and ctas will start to buy.  This feedback loop where it changes in price influence, investor expectations, which influence the narrative, which influences, the price is a form of reflexivity. Reflexivity is a concept from sociology that was transferred over to finance by George Soros. It's a fancier way of saying circularity or feedback loops  As momentum, buying feeds the narrative and feeds on itself. We enter stage 3, the primary trend.  This phase is where the narrative drum beat takes hold, and there are more and more converts lined up behind the apostles from stage 1.  pullbacks or bought, and the News reinforces the price, which reinforces the trend, which reinforces the buying this stage is often, The most profitable in a strong narrative because stage 3 can last a surprisingly, long time.  Just because a narrative is well known. That does not mean it's exhausted.  Stage 3 involves a lot of hype usually from Financial media and tourists who have joined the party fairly early again just because there's hype. Don't think the story is over the hype. Phase can accompany dramatic price moves as demand outstrips supply for an extended period and the market ignores bad news and doubles down on good news.  Stage 4 for first cracks.  At this stage, some cracks appear, bad news hits that threatens The Narrative, the stories crowded and early entrance. Think, maybe the story is over, so they take some profits, there's a meaningful pullback in price. This pullback shakes off the weaker and more recent buyers. But those that believe in the narrative appear on the bid and the price stabilizes slowly, the importance of the bad news dissipates and price climbs back toward the highs.  Stage 5, the final hype wave. As price breaks through and makes new highs. The Narrative often goes into overdrive. Lungs are emboldened by the price action and a strong feeling of fomo comes over anyone. That's not participating.  Depending on the story, you'll often see the narrative crossover from Financial media to the mainstream media. At this point, if the narrative is interesting and important enough.  Non-experts will join the party retail and macro tourists and this will push price aggressively higher. Stage 5 is usually the most surprising and volatile part of the narrative cycle as sellers step away and buyers. Get desperate in Elliot wave. This is also wave 5 and Elliot wave. Theory warns that wave 5 can be the most impulsive part of a move.  This stage is often characterized by choppy jumpy. Volatile moves, Euphoria and overshooting Trend following systems. Add all the way up.  Stage 6, the peak and the turn, the top and the narrative in price can be a long drawn out process. Or an impulsive overshoot and collapse often. It's like the climax in a movie, with a huge flashy, rip higher. Followed by a rapid reversal. That catches everyone on the wrong side. Excellent Traders will realize there's been a meaningful turn in the narrative here. Even when price continues to make new highs, bad Traders, hate to give up on winning trades and great stories and will fall victim to powerful confirmation bias.  As long as price continues to rise, most Traders will ignore information that goes against the narrative. It is a skill to filter out what matters and what does not, of course. Because powerful bull markets, often climb a wall, a bad news.  Eventually price will start to turn and suddenly bad news from 4 hours to 4 weeks ago, pops back into the mind of the market and everyone's like, what the hell am I doing? Still long up here? Things have changed, then everyone reaches for the sell button at once. And boom. It gaps lower and the top is in  quite often the price rise itself will also Change the supply and demand Dynamic, at this stage, there's a saying that the best cure for high prices is high prices, which means that higher prices ultimately, reduce demand and increase Supply and that eventually leads to lower prices.  For example, when the price of oil Rises or Falls gradually consumer and producer Behavior does not change much on the other hand, when the price of oil explodes higher from $50 to $120, we get dated in 2008. Producers rush to bring on new Supply and consumers simultaneously. Cut their demand because they can't afford to fill up their SUVs. They drive less  these shifts in supply and demand are not instantaneous so price can continue to rise. Even as new Supply Works its way into the system and demand disappears.  Then suddenly the new Supply runs into falling demand and prices whoosh lower.  Furthermore, depending on the market the higher price can also have other macroeconomic impacts that will lead to lower price. In the future.  For example, a rise in the Euro might lead to more accommodative ECB policy or a huge ramp up. In Tesla stock, might spur Elon Musk to sell stock in a secondary offering.  Be sure to understand what price levels in your Market might lead to major or nonlinear changes in Supply or demand.  If you're an expert in your market and keenly attuned, to its driving narrative, you'll recognize moments when the story has changed. But price is not yet paying attention. Those can be some of the juiciest setups in finance.  These topics can be false Breaks by highs that happen in a Flash or drawn out periods of distribution that Trace out, triple quintuple or even septuple tops before finally, capitulating lower  When you see a turn in the narrative, you can use your knowledge of technical reversal patterns to help align your strategy with your tactics. That is you can turn bearish near the highs due to a change in the narrative and find high leverage setups that allow you to test your theory. That the narrative is turned without risking a ton, if you're wrong.  Stage 7, the end.  As a narrative expires, people lose interest and only those with bad discipline. Hang on.  Shorts Hammer the price down and long slowly but surely get out Market. Themes are like fashion trends. The Market's interest is fickle and limited people get bored of a theme and they move on sometimes. This will be justified by fundamentals and sometimes it will simply be that a sexier more interesting theme came along.  That is the narrative cycle, I'm describing something clean and visible. But the tricky part is that these Cycles are anything but clean and are only easy to see in hindsight.  As you get experience, though, you'll start to see these Cycles play out in real time and will learn to anticipate and act before the cycle moves to its next stage.  Remember this is not about long-term investing, it's about understanding the current equilibrium. So that as new information arrives, you can capitalize. If you know, where you are in the story and understand current, positioning, you'll have an edge as a cycle. Moves from stage to Stage. These narrative Cycles can last hours days or weeks.  Understand the stories the market is telling itself and learn to identify when the market is falling in or out of love with a narrative.  Let's look at a very simple example of how understanding The Narrative can help you when news comes out. You've been trading the stock of a hydrogen-powered truck manufacturer for a while and has been in a steady uptrend. The market has attached to huge premium to electric and autonomous vehicle stocks and the stock you trade benefits from the halo effect of Tesla.  The main narrative is that autonomous vehicles, have, massive upside, and almost any market cap can be justified.  With this stock, there's a bearish counter narrative humming in the background. That story is that the company has not yet delivered, a single working truck to any customer and behind the scenes, there's Strife inside the company as the founder is viewed by some as a classic Visionary entrepreneur and by others as a marketing, genius, with no technical skill,  In contrast to Tesla, which has produced thousands of working automobiles, The Narrative of the off, the rails. Visionary founder has more potential weight because a company has yet to prove itself capable  A famous short seller comes out with a detailed description of exactly how the founder of the company has used marketing and outright deception to convince people of the company's future success. Despite repeated product failures behind the scenes.  Short sellers come out with reports like this all the time. Some are credible. Some are not some hit the stock on the opening and it rallies back by the end of the day, some are the start of a long slow march to zero. How do you decide what to do?  Obviously, the first thing you do is read the report, you're already an expert on the stock and the narratives that drive it. In this case, you know, the worries about the founder. I've been whispered for a while and therefore, this short sellers report will have more Force than if a comparable report was issued on, say, Tesla.  you sell the stock short on the open and as far as the stock, which opened down 7%, closes down 20%,  Your expert in the stock. So you know, the Market's prior short position has been nearly eliminated over the course of the recent violent uptrend.  You know, from all your reading that the high-profile Bears mostly gave up a few months ago, the chart is a perfect Island reversal day as a stock. Got up to make a new all-time high the day before. The short sellers report came out.  Your stop loss is clear. If the stock goes back above today's open, something is wrong with the idea and you're out.  The first price a security trades after news comes out is called the news pivot. These are critical price points, going forward news, pivots become significant reference points in the mind of the market. And if price, subsequently recaptures, the news pivot, it often means that you Either a, the news wasn't all that important or B, larger medium, and long-term players are using the news as a liquidity event to go the other way.  if bad news comes out and your stock goes back above the news pivot, watch out, we'll talk more about news pivots in chapter 10,  So now you have abrupt flip from bullish, narrative to bearish narrative, large long position almost no shorts textbook Island reversal news, pivot held and stock closed on the lows.  Give it all this, you figure. This is a multi-day trade, today's Tuesday. Knowing that historically, the big impulsive moves in the stock lasts an average of 3 days and considering you don't like to take positions over the weekend. You set your stop-loss at the news, pivot plus 1% and plan to take back the short either. Friday morning, or after another, 15% drop  Naturally you closely Follow The Narrative from here too. And try to assess whether the company's response is to the short sellers report. A credible or maybe just make things worse.  Whether it's a single stock or macroeconomic narrative, you follow. The idea is the same. You must understand the current equilibrium as well as anyone else does. And then when new information comes in or the narrative starts to turn, you're on it. I'll discuss this in more detail shortly in the section on Cross Market correlation Item 7 on my list was technical breakout that's important but don't overestimate the importance of every tech level that you see. There's always another level nearby and not every break of a 10-day high or low or trend line or Fibonacci level has meaning.  If you read about your market and talk to and follow other Traders, you'll start to get a sense for what are the big levels.  A big level isn't a level that's important to you. It's a level that's important to the market. This is a big distinction. You need to have your own views and thoughts, but Alpha Traders are also Inside the Mind of the market and know what matters most to the market as a whole.  This concept is best captured by keen's beauty contest analogy. From the general theory of employment interest and money 1936.  Keen's asks, us to imagine a newspaper which holds a contest where readers are rewarded for choosing which contestant will be chosen by the most other readers.  The task is not to choose the most beautiful woman. But instead to choose the woman, others think is most beautiful. Sometimes that's the same woman but not always.  Participants in complex, multiplayer games with incomplete information, like trading poker, and Bridge play, a superior metagame where they understand, not just their own strategy and preferences but also the strategy and preferences of the other players.  If everyone you talk to is talking about the 122 level and 10 year bond features, you should have that level marked on your charts and you should closely observe how the contract trades around there.  The market converging to the opinion that a particular level is important. Does not happen all that often. But when it does, you should pay attention. If that level breaks, the price action afterwards has important implications for the future, a key level, that breaks and follows through is indicative of a trending Market, while a false break of a level, Everyone's Watching also known as a slingshot reversal is 1 of the best indicators for Trend reversal. Strong uptrends, hold above major support, and they break through major resistance.  Always monitor how your Market behaves at important levels. If a market has been trending and everyone's focused on a particular pullback level that level should hold. If it does not, this may signal the trend is out of gas.  The final Catalyst, in my list was item 8, time of day week or month often, you'll notice that Trends exhaust themselves and turn around at the end of the day, week or month.  For example, in months where the US Stock Market is up 3% or more Sterling dollar frequently rallies into month, end and sells off the first week of the new month. There's a reason for that.  in the PDF, I showed a chart of how Sterling trades around the turn of the month, at the end of the months, where US, Stocks, rallied, 3%, or more,  This is caused by large hedging by Foreign asset managers at month end. Which climaxes at 4 p.m. London time on the last day of the month, note that this impact was huge for Um 2012 to 2021 but has dissipated somewhat since then.  The point of this little section is not to tell you how FX trades on the last day of the month though. If you trade FX, you should know this. It's to show a good example of how time of day week or month can impact price. In this case, the month-end affect determines the direction of the dollar into month end, and then the effect unwinds due to mean reversion in the first week of the following month.  The reason price very often turns at the end of a day week or month, is that many orders in the market are time that way.  A large CTA that needs to buy a million shares of a company will start its vwap at the open and end it at the close.  A large corporation that needs to buy 1 billion. Canada will tell the bank doing the trade to get it done. By the end of the day,  Month. And is a time when many real money investors rebalance so they will complete all their activity. By the end of the day, on the last day of the month and so on.  very, Large orders can often dominate the supply, demand profile of an asset for a short period, most large order, executions take place during the most liquid period of the day.  When that period draws to a close, it's reasonable to assume the supply demand profile for the asset might change. Mean reversion kicks in as a large order is done and the market drifts back toward equilibrium. In other words with the big buyer out of the way, the price can drip lower.  in stocks, there are patterns around the start and end of the month and around options expiring, triple witching Etc,  The takeaway here, is that Trends often mean revert. At the turn of the week, the turn of the month or another key time such as options, expiry. If you're looking to fade a strong Trend, these are often good moments to take a shot. This is an example of something. I think it's important to remember.  There does not have to be an obvious, exogenous Catalyst for every Market move reversal Peak or bottom.  we need to discuss a Great idea with someone and they say, okay, sure about what's the Catalyst? It's okay to say, I don't know.  Not every move has a logical macro Catalyst Market, catalysts are often endogenous as supply and demand change for reasons. Unrelated to macro or fundamentals.  endogenous means having an internal cause or origin, not attributable to any external Factor, the opposite of exogenous  a market high can happen simply because the last buyer bought and the market has run out of buyers or because a single huge buyer is done by as in the Sterling example above  When there are suddenly no buyers in the market, this is called a buyer's strike, a market that was grinding higher for days. Can often plummet suddenly the Catalyst for a sell-off, after an aggressive uptrend or ramp higher can sometimes simply be no more buyers.  Cross Market correlation.  and, Important driver or Catalyst for your Market can be activity and moves in another security or asset class. Try to identify how price action in other markets, may send clues about your Market to take an obvious example. If gold spikes twenty 5 dollars in 3, minutes and Silver's unchanged, would you rather be long or short silver? Probably long.  While correlated products tend to move in sympathy with 1 another, there are usually leads and lags if Apple rips higher on good product launch news can you find the sympathy plays before other Traders do display makers and semiconductor stocks might benefit too for example.  This style of trading where you follow many markets and try to determine which ones are potentially leading or lagging is called intermarket cross Market or lead lag analysis.  This type of analysis. Can give you a significant Edge, though. It has become mainstream in recent years and does not have the edge it once did.  I use cross. Market analysis, all day, every day. Differentials Canadian equities copper gold Etc. And I watched them all closely. I do this by setting up a series of separate charts which overlay the main asset. Each bit of information adds to the last and eventually you have enough. Building blocks stacked up. It results in a solid trade idea.  The more you understand your Market, the better feel you'll get over time as to what it means when divergences build between primary and correlated markets.  I always feel more confident putting on a trade, if the most important correlated variables are stable or moving in my direction,  For example, if I have a strong view that the Canadian dollar is set to Rally based on my economic and technical analysis, but then I see oils just dropped 2 dollars in the past 30 minutes. If I'm about to go short Netflix, but then see Disney and Google, rip higher, a, hold off, and wait for Disney and Google to stabilize. Or for Netflix to catch up before I go short and so on.  Correlated variables, give you a hint as to where your product is going, but not the answer.  As I said earlier, these cross Market relationships often exists because there is a third variable driving to correlated products. For example, gold, in the US dollar have a common driver us real interest rates. If the world thinks the FED is going to do more quantitative easing and push real rates lower you might see that reflected in higher gold first and then a lower dollar shortly after  all other things being equal, lower real rates means stronger, gold and a weaker dollar, but these markets will not all move in perfect sync. Rates in Gold, might be on the move, but a big buyer of US Dollars. May be in the market. So the dollar doesn't move at first. Then when the buyer of dollars is done the dollar catches up Lead lag Traders, look at the entire board and hunt for an overall pattern then they find particular asset prices. That don't make sense and try to get on board before those out of line assets, move back into line  Think of the markets as 1, huge interconnected organism where information influences many assets at once. But not all at the exact same time if a market moves and that market is correlated to yours. Pay attention. What does it mean?  You can use common sense to come up with a list of correlated markets, fairly easily when you read articles about your Market, do they mention other assets? What are some other proxies for Chinese growth that iron ore or the Shanghai Composite Index fxi which is the China a-shares ETF, copper BHP FCX mining stocks Etc  Every in. If you have Bloomberg, you can play around with the Corr and cfnd functions to find things that are correlated to your Market. But it's more useful to use logic rather than data mining.  Start with things that should logically be correlated to your market, then calculate the correlation to see if your guesses are correct.  Use correlation of daily changes, not correlation of levels when calculating correlation.  You can also go to websites like Market topology, calm and mess around with their correlation tools. Just remember to use a logic filter because you can data mine, all kinds of nonsensical correlations if you try hard enough,  Eventually, as you approach expert level in your product slowly Branch out from your market and learn more about a small list of correlated Market.  for example, over many years of trading currencies, I have accumulated a ton of knowledge about markets outside of, but correlated to FX  This knowledge helps me better, understand and trade the currency Market.  For example, if you trade dollar Canada, it's good to know what the Tas order type is in the oil Market because oil is correlated to Dollar Canada. Seek to understand any Market that is correlated to yours search for the most stable, and logical correlations and ignore transitory or nonsensical correlations. Even if they look statistically strong,  A full discussion of lead, like trading could fill an entire book, a study the behavior of correlated. Markets and watch how correlations change over time. Correlations are not static. Sometimes stocks and bonds go up and down together, sometimes they're inversely correlated. Sometimes stocks go up and down with the dollar sometimes, they're inversely correlated.  Closely follow a small list of markets correlated to your primary market and you'll slowly get a feel for how markets move together even as they do not move in perfect sync.  Lead lag is part art and part science. So you should expect that it will take a few years to get a feel for it. Once you do, you will sometimes feel like you can tell what's going to happen before it happens. As you pick up on a strongly correlated leading variable that drives your market for a little while regimes, where lead lag works. Well, can be highly satisfying and profitable trading periods.  Lead lag and correlation trading is not the Holy Grail. It's a popular systematic strategy. Therefore, much of the edge is now captured by computers.  It's an input into a good discretionary trading process. Not an entire discretionary trading process. You can go to Spectre markets.com to sign up.  All right, and now it's time for chapter 10. I understand technicals sentiment, and positioning.  Step 3 towards becoming an expert in your Market.  Success at any complex game puzzle or battle relies on both intelligent strategy and effective tactics.  Tactics are just as important as strategy yet are often overlooked. This chapter explains technical analysis, sentiment and positioning, and shows how Superior understanding of these subjects will give you a significant tactical Edge.  To be clear when it comes to trading, strategy means coming up with trade ideas. And tactics means how you execute and Implement those ideas.  Nearly all my trade ideas start from either micro structure. Or narrative, they never come from looking at a chart or, from any form of technical analysis, and I never put on a trade purely because of sentiment or positioning my highest, conviction trades. Generally have multiple inputs, but they always start with fundamentals or micro structure and then get refined streamlined or filtered out by other factors.  I use technical analysis, sentiment and positioning as timing tools but not as trade selection tools, my thinking, on this, as evolved over the years partly because markets, have evolved. But also, because I've seen enough evidence to conclude that technical analysis is not a source of alpha or Edge that is charts are not a good forecasting tool.  There's a pretty tall pile of research to back this up. I'll give you a quick rundown of 1 paper, called our random strategies more successful than technical ones. They analyze 4 popular technical strategies RSI macd. Mean reversion and momentum.  They ran them against 4, major stock markets, USA, Germany, Italy, and the UK, and compared the results to a strategy that shows entry and exit levels 100% randomly. Here's their conclusion.  Our main result, which is independent of the market considered is that standard trading strategies and their algorithms based on the history of the time series. Although have occasionally the chance to be successful inside small temporal windows on a large temporal scale performance on average, not better than the purely random strategy, which on the other hand is also much less volatile.  In this respect, for the individual Trader, a purely random strategy represents a Costless alternative to expensive professional financial consulting.  being at the same time, also much less risky if compared to the other trading strategies end quote,  There are entire books that go through all the major technical patterns and back test them. And come to the same result. Technical analysis is not a good forecasting tool.  I explained my philosophy on technical analysis when I discussed over Reliance on simple indicators and aoenia.  I want to make it clear, though. I use technical analysis in my trading every day. I just don't use it to predict markets.  Technical analysis gives you an important set of tactical execution and risk management tools. It does not help you forecast Market Direction. Good Traders are quick to admit when they are wrong, they make leverage asymmetric bets, technical analysis can help you do these things better and provide strong and clear signals of when it's time to give up on a bad idea or take profit on a good 1.  Believe it or not, the exact technical indicators, you choose are not all that important.  The mathematics and concept behind most technical indicators are similar RSI macd. And oscillators, I'll give you a similar feel for Trend and momentum Simple trend lines, plus support, and resistance work just as well as more complicated or convoluted methods. The more esoteric you get with your technical analysis. The more likely you are to find yourself wasting time in the weeds. Keep it simple. Remember, Alpha Traders do not use technical analysis to predict the market. They use it to optimize execution and risk management.  Here are some texts. I look at  significant reference points are srps are levels that are likely to feature prominently in the mind of the market. These are levels and majority of Market players view, as important,  As is the case, in keen's beauty contest analogy. It's important to know, not just what you think is important but what others think is important here are some standard significant reference points.  News pivots.  A news pivot is the last trade in the market before important Market. Moving news comes out. I find that news pivots are the Most powerful reference points in trading, you should be aware of all news pivots in your markets. If Apple's at 100 when good news comes out in the first trade. After the news is 103, the Gap is 100 103 with the news pivot 100 marking the bottom of the gap.  Remember that price is a momentary equilibrium determined by the sum of all known information in the universe, when new information, appears the market frantically, tries to find the corresponding new equilibrium. If the market believes the news to be meaningful, it should abandon the old equilibrium from before the news came out, that is the news pivot and find a new 1.  A return to or through the newspaper is important information. It tells you the market has rejected, the news and the attempt to find a new equilibrium and reverted back to its old happy place.  When news comes out the market fumbles around in the dark as it tries to find a new balance between supply and demand buyers and sellers of various time frames. Also, for position, the first price that trades after news comes out is also a significant reference point going forward.  In the PDF, I show an example of a news pivot and price Gap using hyg which is the high yield ETF in the chart. I show of hyg, the High Yield Corporate Bond ETF. You can see that on the day, the FED announced their intention to buy high yield bonds hyg. Gapped massively higher. It closed just below 78 the day before then open at 82, the day of the announcement. Therefore the news pivot, the price. Before the news is 78 and the other side of the Gap is 82. These are both significant reference points going forward.  In the weeks that followed hyg closed, the Gap and retested, the News pivot, the newspaper, held perfectly and hyg, never fell back below 78, despite 4 Separate tests.  astute traders of hyg would be aware of the closing level before the feds announcement and would also be aware that At that level is likely to be massive support.  A simple strategy of buying just above the news, pivot with the stop below. The news pivot would have worked perfectly.  Always take note of the level of any Market. You trade going into a major event. This level is likely to be a significant reference point, going forward.  Support and resistance.  The next type of significant reference point to identify is simple support and resistance if a Stock's in a downtrend, but it starts to build a base and bounces off. 80 3 times, you should take note of this. The beauty of support and resistance are their Simplicity. They are easy to identify and their existence is logical because often they are simple reflection of Supply, or demand at a particular level.  A patient. Medium-term buyer in a market, might decide to work a massive bid at $80 in a stock that massive bid will be visible. As the price repeatedly trades at 80 but fails to break through that level.  Support and resistance. In your Market can be identified, simply by eyeballing both an hourly and a daily chart. Here's what to look for.  1 any top side or downside level that held more than once, this can often be a band, not an exact level. For example, you might see a series of lows and gold at 1914, 1908 and 1904.  Don't use false Precision. We need to determine significant reference points. A band of support often makes more sense than trying to identify an exact level.  2. Any level that represents a major high or low for your product 60-day low. 1 year low Etc  3 broken resistance or support when a major support breaks, expect that level to become important resistance in future.  And 4. Major round numbers. These do not always matter. But remember how humans love round numbers? Would often produce symmetrical patterns.  Furthermore, since volatility tends to be steady in many markets for much of the time, it makes sense, that moves, whether they be up, or down, would tend to be of approximately the same size if something, just rallied 5% in 3 months, its intuitive and logical that it could fall 5% in 3 months but less intuitive or logical than it might fall 12% in 1 month.  Next in the PDF are 2 examples of symmetry that I found useful in September 2020.  The first was dollar Canada.  The co 19 crisis triggered, a massive shortage of dollars as the market scrambled for liquidity and freaked out about the dramatic drop in the stock market.  This push dollar CAD from 12952 to 14669 in Just 2 months.  Then the FED launched. An epic QE bazooka and stocks quickly stabilized.  This helped dollar CAD form a top and eventually, the pair dropped all the way back down to within 30. Tips of where it started 12952. Check out the chart in the PDF. It is a thing of beauty. You can see how dollar Canada, rallied 17.2. Big figures then sold off 16.8 big figures just about perfect symmetry. Pretty amazing.  Once it completed that symmetrical roundtrip dollar CAD held in the mid 129s and that was an excellent buying opportunity. This is not hindsight. I use the Symmetry as 1 of several reasons to recommend a dollar CAD long at the time. The next chart is a thing of absolute Beauty. It shows dollar cnh dollar, China as it, followed a perfect m-shaped, pattern and respected, both price and time symmetry.  Each leg of the M was about 95 days with the first 2. Exactly 999 days. And the last 292 and 97 days. The first rally matched, the ultimate sell-off, just about perfectly and the v in the middle of the M. Displayed just about perfect symmetry too. When an asset is completed, Asymmetrical pattern, you should be on high alert for a reversal.  Furthermore, when trading options you should start with the Assumption of price symmetry, when choosing targets and strike prices, if you're buying dollar cnh puts on the second run to 719, it makes sense to start with 668 as your price Target and then adjust for any other information. You have under Direction and expected volatility.  If you are short dollar cnh at the point the chart ends and you feel like the trade is losing its luster.
